To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed from the graph such that the two black nodes (J and F) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between these two nodes.

1. **Identify the paths between the black nodes (J and F):**
   - Path 1: J -> P -> C -> B -> N -> Y -> O -> P -> I -> C -> B -> N -> B -> H -> K -> X -> F
   - Path 2: J -> D -> P -> C -> B -> N -> Y -> O -> P -> I -> C -> B -> N -> B -> H -> K -> G -> E -> F
   - Path 3: J -> D -> P -> C -> B -> N -> Y -> O -> P -> I -> C -> B -> N -> B -> H -> K -> X -> F

2. **Identify common nodes in these paths:**
   - Common nodes: P, C, B, N, H, K, X

3. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing node B will disconnect all paths that go through B.
   - Removing node H will disconnect paths that go through H.
   - Removing node K will disconnect paths that go through K.

However, removing just one of these nodes (B, H, or K) will not disconnect all paths. We need to remove at least two nodes to ensure complete disconnection.

4. **Optimal cut set:**
   - Removing nodes B and H will disconnect all paths between J and F.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed is 2, with the cut set being [B, H].

**Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, H]**

- However, we need to ensure that other paths are also disconnected.

### Final Cut Set:
